Let's focus algorithms.

Potential new algorithms:
1. Use log-multiplicative updates based on target convolution (like matrix scaling) to flatten b.
2. Use coordinate ascent / block coordinate: update each a_i to minimize max b while sum fixed, with other fixed. For fixed others, b_k as function of x=a_i: b_k = 2x c_k + d_k for k where i involved (c_k = a_{k-i}+? for k-i within range). Objective max( d_k, max_k (d_k+2x c_k) ). Need sum fixed. Coordinate descent exact can be done! This is promising.

For each coordinate i, let S_i = sum of others = 1 - x (if sum fixed to 1). Max over lags involving i and lags not. Function F_i(x)=max( M0_i, max_{lag k in [i, i+n-1?]} d_k + 2 x c_k ), where d_k = current b_k - 2x a_{?} + x^2? Need derive.

b_k = a_i * s_k + q_k, where s_k = sum of a_j over pairs j with i+j=k, j≠i? Since bilinear: if we change a_i from x to y, pairs involving i: b_k = 2 a_i a_{k-i} if i≠k-i, else a_i^2 plus no? Let's derive general.
Given a. For coordinate i, let old x. Pairs (p,q) with p+q=k. If neither p nor q =i, b contributes constant r_k. If one= i:
- if k-i = i (k=2i), original term a_i^2 = x^2.
- if j=k-i ≠i, there is one pair (i,j) and (j,i) for k? In convolution, both terms if both indices in 0..n-1 and i+j=k: sum includes a_i a_j + a_j a_i = 2 x a_j (unless i=j counted once). So for k≠2i, b_k = r_k + 2x a_j where j=k-i in [0,n-1]. For k=2i, b_k = r_k + x^2 where r_k excludes a_i^2 (other pairs with p=q=i only one).
If new y:
- k=2i: r_k + y^2.
- k≠2i: r_k + 2y a_j.
But r_k = old b_k - contribution old (for k=2i: b_old - x^2).
For fixed other variables and sum? If we're doing coordinate update without fixing sum, no. With sum fixed, if update x, to keep sum 1, we would need scale others? Hard. But maybe coordinate descent with sum not fixed and then normalize? Objective scale invariant, can allow sum change but normalize after each coordinate? Or use simplex coordinate move: x changes, distribute negative adjustment evenly to others, complex.

Alternative use log domain and normalize each update. Coordinate update on x while scaling all variables proportionally? Since objective invariant, we can set x and rescale others to sum1. For fixed direction of others, we can scale all to sum1: let other vector O of sum s=1-x. New x', others = O*(1-x')/(1-x). Then b scales? b has terms x'^2, cross 2 x' * scaled a_j, and O scaled^2. Objective scale invariant? If we set sum1, yes. For fixed ratios of others, b as function of x' has form α(x')^2 + β(x') + γ? More manageable.

Coordinate descent on simplex: Fix ratios of all other coordinates, choose x_i to minimize max b with sum1. Let other vector o (length n-1) with positive. Set new a_i = y, others o * (1-y)/(1-x_old?) Actually old sum 1; for optimization we can parameterize by y absolute and scale others to (1-y)/s_old (s_old=∑_{j≠i} a_j =1-x). Then b_k as function of y:
- terms not involving i: (1-y)^2/s^2 * r_k? But r_k computed for old o? Since others scaled by factor λ=(1-y)/s. Terms not involving i scale λ^2.
- cross terms: 2 y λ o_j for j=k-i (if j≠i)
- square: y^2 if k=2i.
Nonlinear but 1D; can find minimum by scanning y in [0,1]. Could be expensive O(n) per coordinate if compute, but maybe use vectorized and ternary? Objective max of quadratics; can find by bisection on t: is there y such that all ≤t? For each k, constraints:
  for k=2i: y^2 ≤ t.
  cross: 2 y (1-y)/s * o_j + (1-y)^2/s^2 r_k ≤ t.
  no cross: (1-y)^2/s^2 r_k ≤ t.
Solving many quadratics; maybe too complex.

Simpler: Use gradient/line-search in log domain. We can also use coordinate " power method" with ratios.

Alternative: Use "linearized" model around current a: F(a)=max b. Since b=(a+δ)^2 ≈ b + 2 a∘? δ. We can solve s.t. new b≤T by quadratic linearization and nonnegative? Maybe a_i update via linear constraints? For chosen T, minimize distance to current a subject to a≥0,sum=1, and linearized b_k = b_k + 2∑_a? δ ≤ T. This is convex! def em_update(a, n, target='flat', alpha=0.5, T=None, rng=None):
  b=conv(a,a)
  if target flat:
    w = 1.0/(len(b)) maybe or normalized unique? We want sum target = sum b =1. Since a sum1. b.sum ≈1. Let M=len(b); w.fill(1/M).
  peak target:
    T = maybe current max * (1 - shrink?) or fixed. w = b.copy(); w = np.minimum(w, T); if sum small, fallback flat; normalize w to sum1.
    This makes high b reduced, low unchanged.
  ratio = w / (b + eps)
  # H_i = crosscorr(a, ratio) (using convolution cross positive lags)
  H = cross_corr(a, ratio, n) ; length n
  mult = H / H.mean()? Damping: a_new = a * (mult / mult.mean())^eta? Or a_new = a * exp(eta*(logH - meanlogH)). Since H may be large; use logs.
  logH = log(H+eps); shift; exp(eta*(logH - mean)); a_*; normalize sum1.
  return a_new, bmax, etc.

### FFT convolution

```
def fft_conv_self(a):
    n=a.size
    size=1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()
    F=scipy_fft.fft(a, size)
    b=np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(F*F))
    return b[:2*n-1]
```
Use `scipy.fft.fft` or numpy.fft; numpy fine. Could use real FFT? Since a real and self-convolution symmetric? Not necessarily symmetric. Use complex.

To speed, can preallocate? No.

Objective:
```
def sequence_objective(a):
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    n=a.size
    if s<0.01: return inf
    b=fft_conv_self(a)
    return float(2*n*b.max()/(s*s))
```
For normalized sum=1, same.

```
def lse_weights_and_grad_a(a, beta):
   b=fft_conv_self(a)
   c=b-b.max()
   # cap
   scaled=beta*c; if max>500 reduce
   exp=np.exp(scaled); sum=exp.sum(); w=exp/sum
   # G_i = 2 cross_corr(a,w)
   L=1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()
   Aha=scipy_fft.fft(a, L)
   Wha=scipy_fft.fft(w, L)
   G=2*np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(np.conj(Aha)*Wha))[:n]
   return b, w, G
```
Check cross formula: c_i=∑_j a_j w_{i+j}; yes G_i=2c_i.

Objective:
```
def full_lse_objective(z, n, beta, eps_shift):
    a = softmax_stable(z)
    b = conv
    c = b - b.max()
    # stable LSE
    val = b.max() + logsumexp(beta*c)/beta
    val += eps_shift * (np.mean(z)**2)
    return val
```

def line_search_projected(a, step, H):
  f0 = hβ(a) (we have b maybe)
  x = project_simplex(a - step*H, 1)
  f1 = hβ(x)
  c=1e-4

```

### Fourier mutations

For basin hopping:
`fourier_phase_mutate(a, scale)`: compute FFT of length 2n-1 (or fsize), random phase noise with decaying? Previous. Use scale ~0.1-0.5. Project simplex to sum1. This might produce diverse sequences. Maybe use multiplicative random perturbation: a *= exp(noise), normalize. Noise smooth in index (Fourier low freq). Simpler:
```
def smooth_random_mutate(a, rng, scale):
  n = len(a)
  # random Fourier amplitudes low frequencies
  fsize=nextpow2(n)
  mag = np.zeros(fsize); for k in 1..min(20,n/4): mag[k]=mag[-k]=rng.normal(0,scale)
  noise=real(ifft(mag))
  a_new = a * exp(noise) (or a+scale*noise)
  project_simplex
```
This maintains positivity. Maybe better than phase.

Also random subset mask: zero out some parts? But nonnegative; sparse may hurt.

def lbfgs_polish(a, n, time_budget, beta_values=(30,150,800), maxiter_per=40):
    a = normalize_to_sum(a,1)
    z = log_a(z0)
    for beta in beta_values:
       if time.monotonic()>start+time_budget-10: break
       res = minimize(full_lse_fun, z, jac=full_lse_jac, method='L-BFGS-B', args=(n,beta,1e-10), options={'maxiter': maxiter_per, 'ftol':1e-14, 'gtol':1e-9, 'maxfun': 500})
       if res.x finite: z=res.x; a=softmax(z); normalize
    return a
```

### Sequence resampling

Implement `resample_sum1(a, n_out)`:
```
def resample_sum1(a, n_out):
    a = np.asarray(a); n_in=a.size
    if n_in == n_out: s=a.sum(); return a/s if s>0 else uniform
    # Use interpolation of cumulative? For nonnegative, linear interpolation and normalize.
    x_in=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_in)
    x_out=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_out)
    b=np.interp(x_out, x_in, a)
    b=np.clip(b,0,None)
    s=b.sum()
    if s<=0: b=np.full(n_out,1.0/n_out)
    else: b/=s
    return b
```
For downsample, this oversamples edge but okay. For block average (pooling) maybe better: If out divides in, average blocks. Could implement both:
```
if n_out < n_in and n_in % n_out ==0: b=a.reshape(n_out,n_in//n_out).mean(axis=1)
```
But if not, interpolation. Good.

### Multiplicative EM implementation more concretely

```
def cross_corr_a_r(a, r):
    n=a.size
    L=1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()
    Af=scipy_fft.fft(a, L)
    Rf=scipy_fft.fft(r, L)
    return np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(np.conj(Af)*Rf))[:n]
```
Need ensure r length maybe 2n-1. If r longer? pad. Use `r` array.

`em_step(a, mode, eta, T=None)`:
```
n=a.size
b=fft_conv_self(a)
bmax=b.max()
M=2*n-1
if mode == 0: # flat
   w=np.full(M, 1.0/M)
elif mode == 1: # capped
   if T is None: T=max(bmax*0.97, b.mean()+0.5*(bmax-b.mean()))
   w=np.minimum(b, T)
   ws=w.sum(); if ws<1e-15: fallback flat; else w/=ws
elif mode == 2: # inverse flatten
   eps=1e-12*max(bmax,1e-300)
   w=1.0/(b+eps); w/=w.sum()
```
But for flat target, w sum=1; for capped, w sum maybe <1, normalize to 1 (objective target total sum1). For normalized a, b sum should=1 (within floating). Use `nrm = w.sum(); if nrm>0: w/=nrm`.

`ratio = w / (b + eps)`, eps maybe `1e-15 * bmax` or `np.finfo(float).eps * bmax * 10`. If b zero, ratio =w/eps huge; could cause H huge. Use `eps = max(1e-18 * max(bmax,1.0), 1e-300)`. Since b scale 1/n ~6e-5, eps=1e-23? Maybe too small; if b=0 due underflow, ratio large. We can floor b at `eps = max(1e-12 * bmax, 1e-300)`. That avoids extremely large. Flat target: ratio ~1/(n/bmax) ~ n? H ~ crosscorr a*ratio: a~1/n, ratio~n? H~O(1)? Good.

Compute H = cross_corr_a_r(a, ratio). If H has negative small due numerical, clip to 0? It should be >=0 if a>=0 and ratio>=0; FFT roundoff small. `H=np.maximum(H,0)`. If sum tiny, return a.

     accepted=False
     for _ in range(3):

        obj=sequence_objective(a_new)
        if obj < cur - 1e-15:

        # recompute cur

        if cur<best_obj: update
        eta=0.2
        last_improve? 
     if iter-last_improve>1000: break
  return best,best_obj
```

### Smooth mutation

```
def smooth_mutation(a, rng, scale):
    n=len(a)
    fsize=1 << (n-1).bit_length()
    F=np.zeros(fsize, complex)
    n_modes=min(50, fsize//4)
    for k in range(1,n_modes+1):
        amp = rng.normal(0, scale / (1+0.2*k))
        F[k]=amp + 1j*rng.normal(0, scale/(1+0.2*k))
        F[-k]=np.conj(F[k])
    noise=np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(F,fsize))[:n]
    noise = noise - noise.mean()
    a_new = a * np.exp(np.clip(noise, -10,10))
    return project_simplex(a_new, 1)
```
Loop over modes 50 okay. Could vectorize but fine. Need no closures.

```
def phase_mutation(a, rng, scale): ...
```

`lbfgs_polish`:
```
def lbfgs_polish(a, n, time_budget, betas=None, maxiter=40, maxfun=300):
    start=time.monotonic()
    a=normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
    cur=sequence_objective(a); best=a.copy()
    z=z_from_a(a)
    if betas is None:
       betas=[max(100,n//5), max(200,n), max(1000,10*n)]
    for beta in betas:
       if time.monotonic() > start+time_budget-15: break
       try:
           res = minimize(full_lse_fun, z, jac=full_lse_jac, method='L-BFGS-B', args=(n,float(beta),1e-12), options={'maxiter':maxiter,'gtol':1e-10,'ftol':1e-15,'maxfun':maxfun,'disp':False})
       except Exception:
           break
       if res.x is not None and finite:
           a_new=softmax_from_z(res.x)
           a_new=normalize_to_sum(a_new,1.0)
           obj=sequence_objective(a_new)
           if obj<cur:
              a=a_new; cur=obj; best=a.copy(); z=z_from_a(a)
           else:
              # still update z to result? maybe not if worse
              pass
    return best
```
If beta values too high and L-BFGS fails, returns best.

`z_from_a`:
```
def z_from_a(a, floor=1e-12):
   a=np.asarray(a)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=0: a=np.ones_like(a)/len(a)
   else: a=a/s
   loga=np.log(np.clip(a, floor, None))
   loga -= loga.mean()
   loga = np.clip(loga, -40.0, 40.0)
   return loga
```
Clipping logs changes a; normalize after z transform in polish.

Potential issue: `z` mean zero but after clipping, mean may not zero; okay.

```
def active_peak_polish(a, time_budget, k=16):
    a=normalize_sum1(a)
    cur=sequence_objective(a)
    best=a.copy(); bestobj=cur
    step=1.0
    start=time.monotonic()
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()<start+time_budget:
       b=fft_conv_self(a)
       bmax=b.max()
       mean=b.mean()
       idx=np.argsort(b)[::-1][:k]
       vals=b[idx]
       floor=min(vals.min(), bmax - 1e-12)
       weights=np.maximum(vals - floor, 1e-300)
       if weights.sum()<1e-30: weights=np.ones(k)/k
       weights/=weights.sum()
       w_full=np.zeros(2*n-1); w_full[idx]=weights
       s=2.0*cross_corr_a_r(a,w_full)
       accepted=False
       for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625):
          x=project_simplex(a - mult*step*s, 1.0)
          obj=sequence_objective(x)
          if obj < cur - 1e-16:
             a=x; cur=obj; best=a.copy(); accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
          # try smaller step overall
          step *=0.5
          if step<1e-6 or no_improve>50: break
          no_improve+=1
       else:
          no_improve=0
    return best,bestobj
```

`lp_direction` implementation details:
```
def lp_direction(a_fine, n_c=512):
    try:
        n_f=a_fine.size
        if n_f < n_c: return None
        a_c = resample_sum1(a_fine, n_c)
        b_c = fft_conv_self(a_c)
        M = float(b_c.max())
        if M <= 0 or not finite: return None
        N=n_c
        A = np.zeros((2*N-1, N), dtype=np.float64)
        # vectorized: maybe use for loop columns
        for j in range(N):
            A[j:j+N, j] = a_c
        # b vector length 2N-1 = M
        c = -np.ones(N)
        try:
            res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*N-1, M), bounds=[(0,None)]*N, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
        except Exception:
            return None
        if getattr(res,'success',False):
            g=res.x
            if g is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
                g=np.maximum(g,0); s=g.sum()
                if s>0:
                    g/=s
                    gf=resample_sum1(g, n_f)
                    return gf
        return None
    except Exception:
        return None
```
`bounds=(0, None)` can be `bounds=(0, None)` instead of list? linprog accepts sequence. Use `bounds=(0.0, None)`? In linprog, `bounds` sequence of (min,max) for each variable; or `bounds=[(0,None)]*N`. Good. No lambda.

Dense A for N=512 allocated each call; okay. memory.

### Testing mental for L-BFGS functions

`full_lse_jac` gradient: Let's verify softmax gradient with full z no fixed, plus reg. For h = LSE(b; weights), G_i = ∑_k w_k ∂b_k/∂a_i = 2∑_j w_{i+j}a_j. cross_corr gives C_i =∑_j a_j w_{i+j}. symmetric multiplication; yes. dot=∑ a_i G_i. Gradient wrt z = a*(G - dot). Because ∂a_i/∂z_j = a_i(δij - a_j). Then ∑ F_a_i ∂a_i/∂z_j = a_j(F_a_j - ∑ a_i F_a_i). F_a=G. Good. If we use full softmax without fixing, gradient has sum0. Add reg mean. Good.

`full_lse_fun` objective value: LSE of b. Is h = (1/β) log ∑ exp(β b). We compute `bmax + log(s)/beta_eff` where s=∑exp(β(c-cmax? c=b-bmax; cmax=0). Good. If all b equal, s=M, logM/beta. As β→∞, logM/β→0. Good. For line search, constants small.

Beta_eff chosen: If maxc very small, beta_eff=beta (unless beta*maxc <50). Use `max_possible = maxc*beta`; if max_possible>50: beta_eff=50/maxc else beta. If maxc=0, beta_eff=beta.

Need avoid division by zero if maxc < tiny; use if maxc < 1e-300: weights uniform; lse_val=bmax. Else.

`weights` length 2n-1. `cross_corr_a_r` uses FFT size `1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()`. For n=1? Not used; n large.

Potential memory: `full_lse_jac` allocates z? no. `fft_conv_self` uses complex arrays size 32768, fine.

### `project_simplex` implementation

Standard with sort. Need ensure returns sum S within tolerance.
```
def project_simplex(v, S):
    v=np.asarray(v,float)
    n=v.size
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css=np.cumsum(u)
    rho_cond = u - (css - S)/np.arange(1,n+1) > 0
    if not np.any(rho_cond):
       theta = v.mean()
    else:
       tmp = rho_cond * v  #? Standard
       idx = np.where(rho_cond)[0][0]
       theta = (css[idx] - S)/(idx+1)
    w=np.maximum(v-theta,0)
    # If sum not S due rounding, adjust:
    s=w.sum()
    if s>0: w *= S/s
    return w
```
But standard formula: valid = u > (css - S)/idx. The rho index is min? Let's implement known:
```
w = np.sort(v)[::-1]
css = np.cumsum(w)
n = len(w)
cond = w - (css - S)/np.arange(1,n+1) > 0
if np.any(cond):
    i = np.where(cond)[0][0]  # first? Actually choose max j satisfying; with descending, the valid are prefix? Use int(np.max(np.where(cond)[0]))+1.
    theta=(css[i-1]-S)/i
else:
    theta=v.mean()
```
Need correct. Standard from wiki:
```
v = np.sort(v)[::-1]
r = np.arange(1,n+1)
f = (v - S/r) > 0
tmp = f.cumsum() - 1
inds = v - (np.cumsum(v)-S)/r
rho = inds[tmp>0?].hmm.
```
Let's implement robust bisection? Simpler: Since we often project v that are already close to simplex, we can use iterative bisection for theta: find theta such that sum(max(v-theta,0))=S. For v finite. theta in [min(v), max(v)-S/n?]. Binary 60 iterations O(n) each; for n=16k, 60*16k=1M ops per projection, okay but if many projections maybe slower. Sorting O(n logn) in C faster. Use standard reliable:
```
def project_simplex(v, S):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=float)
    if v.size == 0: return v
    n = v.size
    # If S <= 0: return zeros
    if S <= 0: return np.zeros_like(v)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n+1, dtype=float)
    # find largest rho such that u[rho] > (css[rho]-S)/rho
    cond = u > (css - S) / idx
    if not np.any(cond):
        theta = v.mean()
    else:
        # cond should be true for initial segment? Use last true index
        rho = int(np.max(np.where(cond)[0]) + 1)
        theta = (css[rho-1] - S) / rho
    w = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = w.sum()
    if s > 0 and not np.isclose(s, S, rtol=1e-12, atol=1e-15):
        w = w * (S / s)
    return w
```
Test: v=[3,2,1], S=2. sort [3,2,1], css [3,5,6], idx [1,2,3], cond: 3>1 true, 2>1.5 true, 1>1.333 false. rho=2 theta=(5-2)/2=1.5, w=[1.5,0.5,0] sum2. Good. v=[0,0,0], S=1: cond? u 0 > (0-1)/1 negative true? all true? rho=3 theta=(0-1)/3=-1/3, w=1/3 each sum1. Good. If all negative? works.

`normalize_to_sum` can just clip and scale, which is faster. For EM we use normalize.

### `resample_sum1` with block average and preserve endpoints

```
def resample_sum1(a, n_out):
    a=np.clip(np.asarray(a,float),0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return np.full(n_out,1.0/n_out)
    a=a/s
    if a.size == n_out: return a
    if n_out < a.size and a.size % n_out == 0:
        b=a.reshape(n_out, a.size//n_out).mean(axis=1)
    else:
        x_in=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size)
        x_out=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_out)
        b=np.interp(x_out, x_in, a)
        b=np.clip(b,0,None)
    s=b.sum()
    if s<=0: b=np.full(n_out,1.0/n_out)
    else: b=b/s
    return b
```
If downsampling by averaging, sum output =1/n? Actually block averages sum to1; normalize again. Good. If upsampling, interpolation sum maybe approx 1; normalize.

### `mix_two`
```
def mix_two(a,b,t):
   a=normalize_to_sum(a,1); b=normalize_to_sum(b,1)
   c=(1-t)*a + t*b
   return normalize_to_sum(c,1)
```

### More on `em_optimize` acceptance and objective

Let's implement with fixed iterations based on time budget:
```
def em_optimize(a, time_budget, rng=None):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max(5.0,time_budget)
    a=normalize_to_sum(a,1)
    cur=sequence_objective(a)
    best=a.copy(); bestobj=cur
    eta=0.25
    last_improve=0
    it=0
    mode=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
       it+=1
       mode=(it-1)%3
       accepted=False
       T=None
       # compute old b for T? em_step computes. For capped need T, compute bmax=fft_conv_self(a).max()
       b=fft_conv_self(a)
       bmax=b.max()
       if mode==1:
           T=max(1e-12, bmax*(1.0 - 0.08))
       # try eta steps
       for _ in range(4):
           a_new = em_step(a, mode, eta, T)
           obj=sequence_objective(a_new)
           if obj <= cur - 1e-16:
              a=a_new; cur=obj
              if obj<bestobj: best=a.copy(); bestobj=obj; last_improve=it
              accepted=True; break
           else:
              eta = max(0.02, eta*0.5)
       if accepted:
          eta = min(0.6, eta + 0.02)
       else:
          # perturb small, maybe try mode 0 small
          a_p = smooth_mutation(a, rng, 0.03) if rng is not None else a
          objp=sequence_objective(a_p)
          if objp < cur:
             a=a_p; cur=objp
          # reduce eta and break after stagnation
          if it - last_improve > 200: break
    return best,bestobj
```
But if no accept, we don't move best, may loop same. We could accept a small increase if it helps escape? For local EM, no. Stagnation break. To avoid too quick, perturb and accept under annealing: if objp < bestobj + 1e-8 maybe. But keep best only if improves. If cur worsens, best unchanged. Next iterations from a different a. Good.

Need if rng None, deterministic? Pass seed. In EM perturbation, use rng; if None, no.

```
def em_step_from_b(a,b,mode,eta,T=None):
    n=a.size; M=2*n-1
    bmax=float(b.max())
    if bmax < 1e-300: return normalize_to_sum(a,1)
    if mode==0:
       w=np.full(M,1.0/M)
    elif mode==1:
       if T is None: T=max(bmax*(1.0-0.05), b.mean()+0.25*(bmax-b.mean()))
       w=np.minimum(b, T)
       ws=w.sum()
       if ws<1e-12: w=np.full(M,1.0/M)
       else: w=w/ws
    else:
       floor=max(1e-10*bmax,1e-300)
       w=1.0/(np.maximum(b,floor)+0.05*bmax)
       wsum=w.sum(); if wsum>0: w/=wsum
    floor=max(1e-10*bmax,1e-300)
    ratio=w/(np.maximum(b,floor))
    H=cross_corr_a_r(a,ratio)
    H=np.maximum(H,0.0)
    if H.sum() <=0: return normalize_to_sum(a,1)
    logH=np.log(np.maximum(H,1e-300))
    # use weighted geometric mean by a? Just mean
    logmean=logH.mean()
    fac=np.exp(np.clip(eta*(logH-logmean), -30.0, 30.0))
    a_new=a*fac
    return normalize_to_sum(a_new,1.0)
```
Maybe for flat target inverse mode floor 1e-10 bmax; if b very small, ratio capped. Good.

`mode 2 inverse` maybe too strong; use `floor=0.05*bmax` in denominator to cap low. Good.

`em_optimize` can call `em_step_from_b` with b.

### phase_mutation implementation

```
def phase_mutation(a, rng, scale):
    n=a.size; fsize=1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()
    F=scipy_fft.fft(a,fsize)
    mag=np.abs(F); phase=np.angle(F)
    freqs=np.arange(fsize,dtype=float)
    weight=1.0 + freqs/(0.1*fsize)
    noise=rng.normal(0.0, scale*weight, fsize)
    Fnew=mag*np.exp(1j*(phase+noise))
    # preserve DC sum
    Fnew[0]=F[0]
    a_new=np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(Fnew,fsize))[:n]
    return project_simplex(a_new,1.0)
```
Random scale maybe 0.2. This can produce negative but projection. Good.

### smooth_mutation vectorized

```
def smooth_mutation(a, rng, scale):
    n=a.size
    fsize=1 << (n-1).bit_length()
    F=np.zeros(fsize, dtype=complex)
    max_mode=min(fsize//4, 64)
    if max_mode>1:
       ks=np.arange(1,max_mode+1)
       amps=rng.normal(0.0, scale/(1.0+0.05*ks), max_mode)
       phs=rng.uniform(0.0,2*np.pi,max_mode)
       F[ks]=amps*np.exp(1j*phs)
       F[-ks]=np.conj(F[ks])
    noise=np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(F,fsize))[:n]
    noise=noise-noise.mean()
    noise=np.clip(noise, -10,10)
    a_new=a*np.exp(noise)
    return project_simplex(a_new,1.0)
```
Use vector arrays; no loops (except none). Good.

### `make_initials` not too many to keep sorting simple

We'll create list `inits=[]` and append. Need avoid appending duplicates? Not.

Power-law uses `np.power(x+eps, -alpha)` with alpha maybe 1.5; endpoints eps=1e-6; normalized. Good.

Geometric: `x = np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)`. For `exp(-lam*x)` and reverse. Also `exp(lam*(x-0.5))` gives high at end; reverse covers low at end. `exp(-lam*(x-0.5))` high at center; `exp(lam*abs(x-0.5))` high at ends; `exp(-lam*abs(x-0.5))` center peak. Normalize.

Some initial may have zeros? no.

### Direct test of objective constant for n: constant a=1/n, bmax=1/n? For full conv of constant length n: b_k = (k+1)/n^2 for k<n, (2n-1-k)/n^2; max=n/n^2=1/n. obj=2n*1/n=2. Good.

Geometric asymptotic ~1.8. Optimizer should improve.

### `normalize_to_sum`:
```
def normalize_to_sum(a, S=1.0):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
   a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=0 or not finite: return np.full(a.size, S/a.size if a.size else 1)
   return a*(S/s)
```
If size 0, return empty? But we won't.

### `fft_conv_self`:
```
def fft_conv_self(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
    size=1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()
    F=scipy_fft.fft(a,size)
    b=scipy_fft.ifft(F*F)
    b=np.real(b)
    return b[:2*n-1]
```
Potential small negative b at edges due roundoff; objective max could be negative tiny? b nonnegative; use `np.maximum(b,0)` maybe changes sum but okay. `b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)` to avoid negative in EM. For objective, max unaffected? If all small? Use.

### `full_lse_fun` with `compute_lse_parts` returning None
```
def compute_lse_parts(z, n, beta):
    if z is None or len(z)!=n: return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)): return None
    span = z.max() - z.min()
    if span > 800.0: return None
    a=softmax_from_z(z)
    b=fft_conv_self(a)
    bmax=float(b.max())
    M=len(b)
    c=b-bmax
    maxc=float(c.max())
    if maxc < 1e-300:
       weights=np.full(M,1.0/M)
       lse=bmax  # plus logM/beta negligible
       beta_eff=beta
    else:
       beta_eff=beta
       if beta_eff*maxc > 50.0:
           beta_eff=50.0/maxc
       scaled=beta_eff*c
       # if scaled max not 0 due, okay; min maybe -large
       ex=np.exp(np.clip(scaled, -50.0, 50.0)) # c<=0 so scaled<=0, no overflow; if scaled>50 not after beta_eff, but clip for safety
       s=ex.sum()
       if s<=0 or not finite: weights uniform; lse=bmax
       else: weights=ex/s; lse=bmax + np.log(s)/beta_eff
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(weights)) or not np.isfinite(lse): return None
    return a,b,bmax,weights,lse
```
Actually c in [neg,0], scaled <=0; no need clip upper. Use if beta_eff*maxc >50 ensures max scaled=50. Good.

`softmax_from_z`:
```
def softmax_from_z(z):
    z=np.asarray(z,dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)): return np.ones(len(z))/len(z)
    m=z.max()
    p=np.exp(z-m)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): return np.ones(len(z))/len(z)
    return p/s
```

`full_lse_jac`: if parts None return zeros. Add reg derivative.

def optimize_quick(a, time_budget, n, rng):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max(5.0,time_budget)
    a=normalize_to_sum(a,1)
    best_a=a; best_o=sequence_objective(a)
    t=min(15.0, time_budget*0.4)
    if time.monotonic()+t < deadline:
       a1,o1=em_optimize(a1,t,rng); update
    t=min(20.0, max(5.0,(deadline-now)*0.5))
    if t>8:
       betas=[int(max(500, 10*n))]
       a2,o2=lbfgs_polish(a,t,n,betas=betas,maxiter=15,maxfun=120)
       update
    # active polish remaining
    rem=deadline-now
    if rem>2:
       a3,o3=active_peak_polish(a, rem, k=12)
       update
    return best_a,best_o
```

### `active_peak_polish` top-level signature

```
def active_peak_polish(a, time_budget, k=16):
   start...
   a=normalize_to_sum(a,1)
   best_a=a.copy(); best_o=sequence_objective(a)
   cur=best_o
   step=1.0
   it=0
   while time.monotonic()<start+max(2,time_budget)-1:
       it+=1
       b=fft_conv_self(a)
       bmax=b.max()
       if bmax<=0: break
       k_use=min(k, 2*a.size-1)
       idx=np.argsort(b)[::-1][:k_use]
       vals=b[idx]
       floor = min(vals.min(), bmax - 1e-12)
       wv=np.maximum(vals-floor, 1e-300)
       ws=wv.sum()
       if ws<1e-300:
           wv=np.ones(k_use)/k_use
       else:
           wv=wv/ws
       wf=np.zeros(2*a.size-1)
       wf[idx]=wv
       g=2.0*cross_corr_a_r(a, wf)
       accepted=False
       for mult in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625):
           x=project_simplex(a - mult*step*g, 1.0)
           ox=sequence_objective(x)
           if ox < cur - 1e-16:
               a=x; cur=ox
               if ox<best_o: best_o=ox; best_a=a.copy()
               accepted=True
               break
       if not accepted:
           step*=0.5
           if step<1e-5 or it>200: break
           # maybe increase k
           k=min(64,k*2)
   return best_a,best_o
```
If no accept for many iterations, break. Could stagnation but okay.

### `em_optimize` with modes

Need pass time_budget. In EM, computing `fft_conv_self` for b and then `sequence_objective` for a_new, so each try 2 FFT. If eta small, may not improve. Stagnation break after 100 no accept. For time 30s, okay.

```
def em_optimize(a, time_budget, rng=None):
   start... deadline=start+max(5,time_budget)
   a=normalize...
   cur=sequence_objective(a)
   best...
   eta=0.25
   it=0
   no_acc=0
   while now < deadline-2:
      it+=1
      b=fft_conv_self(a)
      mode=(it-1)%3
      T=None
      bmax=b.max()
      if mode==1 and bmax>0:
          T=max(bmax*(1.0-0.05), 0.5*(b.max()+b.mean()))? Actually bmean maybe < T. Use T=max(0.95*bmax, b.mean()+0.2*(bmax-b.mean()))
      accepted=False
      for _ in range(3):
         a_new=em_step_from_b(a,b,mode,eta,T)
         obj=sequence_objective(a_new)
         if obj <= cur - 1e-16:
            a=a_new; cur=obj; if obj<best...; accepted=True; break
         eta=max(0.02, eta*0.5)
      if accepted:
         eta=min(0.7, eta+0.03); no_acc=0
      else:
         no_acc +=1
         if rng is not None and it%20==0:
            a_p=smooth_mutation(a,rng,0.03)
            objp=sequence_objective(a_p)
            if objp < best_o + 1e-10:
                 a=a_p; cur=objp  # allow slight worsening of current but not best
         if no_acc > 100: break
   return best
```
If no accept, eta reduced to 0.02 next iteration. We should reset eta maybe 0.2 after perturbation. Use `eta=0.2` if perturbed. Good.

### `em_step_from_b` inverse mode

Let's set mode 0 flat, 1 capped, 2 inverse. For capped T: choose `T = max(0.97*bmax, bmean + 0.5*(bmax-bmean))` but if bmean+... > bmax? no. Simpler `T = bmax*(1.0 - 0.05)`. If bmax close, okay. If T < bmin? w sum maybe high. Good.

### `make_initials` detailed code

Need avoid too many inits causing top sort with full fallback. Let's limit 40.

```
def make_initials(n, given, rng):
   inits=[]
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
   if given is not None:
       g=np.clip(np.asarray(given,float),0,1000)
       s=g.sum()
       if s>0:
          g=g/s
          inits.append(g if g.size==n else resample_sum1(g,n))
          if g.size != n and n % g.size == 0:
              inits.append(normalize_to_sum(np.repeat(g,n//g.size),1))
   inits.append(np.full(n,1.0/n))
   lams=[0.3,0.7,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0,5.0]
   for lam in lams:
       a=np.exp(-lam*x); inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1)); inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a[::-1],1))
       a=np.exp(lam*(x-0.5)); inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1)); inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a[::-1],1))
       a=np.exp(lam*np.abs(x-0.5)); inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1))
       a=np.exp(-lam*np.abs(x-0.5)); inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1))
   for a in ... maybe too many (35). okay.
   pows=[0.2,0.5,0.8]
   eps=1e-6
   for p in pows:
       a=(x+eps)**(-p)+((1-x)+eps)**(-p); inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1))
       a=(x+eps)**p*((1-x)+eps)**p; inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1))
   # windows
   for p in [0.5,1,2,4]:
       a=np.sin(np.pi*x)**p + 1e-6; inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1))
   a=0.5+0.5*np.cos(2*np.pi*x); inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1))
   a=0.5-0.5*np.cos(2*np.pi*x); inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1))
   # random smooth 4
   for _ in range(4):
      a=smooth_signal(rng,n,base=1.0, scale=0.3)
      inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a,1))
   # random block
   for nb in [8,16,32]:
      bl=np.maximum(rng.normal(1.0,0.4, nb),0.05)
      a=np.repeat(bl, n//nb)
      if len(a)<n: a=np.concatenate((a, np.full(n-len(a), bl[-1])))
      inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a[:n],1))
   return inits
```

### `smooth_positive_signal` code

```
def smooth_positive_signal(n, rng, base=1.0, scale=0.2):
    fsize=1 << (n-1).bit_length()
    F=np.zeros(fsize, dtype=complex)
    max_mode=min(fsize//4, 80)
    if max_mode > 1:
       ks=np.arange(1,max_mode+1)
       amps=rng.normal(0.0, scale/(1.0+0.1*ks), max_mode)
       phs=rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*np.pi, max_mode)
       F[ks]=amps*np.exp(1j*phs)
       F[-ks]=np.conj(F[ks])
    noise=np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(F, fsize))[:n]
    noise=noise-noise.mean()
    return np.maximum(base + scale*noise, 0.01)
```

### Code details for `lbfgs_polish`

```
def lbfgs_polish(a, n, time_budget, betas=None, maxiter=30, maxfun=250):
    a=normalize_to_sum(a,1.0)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max(5.0,time_budget)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_o=sequence_objective(a)
    z=z_from_a(a)
    if betas is None:
       betas=[int(max(50,n//4)), int(max(200,n)), int(max(500,10*n))]
    for beta in betas:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 15: break
       try:
           res = minimize(full_lse_fun, z, jac=full_lse_jac, method='L-BFGS-B',
                          args=(n, float(beta), 1e-12),
                          options={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'maxfun': int(maxfun), 'ftol':1e-15, 'gtol':1e-10, 'disp':False})
       except Exception:
           break
       x = getattr(res, 'x', None)
       if x is None: continue
       a_new = softmax_from_z(np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64))
       a_new = normalize_to_sum(a_new,1.0)
       o_new=sequence_objective(a_new)
       if np.isfinite(o_new) and o_new < best_o:
           best_a=a_new.copy(); best_o=o_new
           z=z_from_a(a_new)
       else:
           # maybe don't change z
           pass
    return best_a, best_o
```

Potential problem: In `full_lse_jac`, using `np.mean(z)` if z is list? It is ndarray. Good.

Potential problem: `minimize` might pass `z` as `np.ndarray` but if `fun` returns `np.inf` and `jac` zeros, line search may think no progress. okay.

Potential problem: In `lbfgs_polish`, `betas` passed e.g. ints; `for beta in betas`. If `betas` is tuple, okay.

Potential problem: `optimize_candidate` uses `betas_medium = [int(max(50, n // 4)), int(max(200, n)), int(max(500, 10 * n))]`. For n=16384, includes 4096,16384,163840. Good. For high: [int(max(500,20*n))]=327680.

### More math on EM inverse mode: If mode 2 target inverse gives low lags high target; may increase endpoints too much. But acceptance protects. Good.

### Maybe we should include `mode` order flat -> capped -> inverse. Yes.

### If EM objective doesn't improve from previous best, after 100 no accept break. For a good local optimum, quick.

### In `em_optimize`, after no accept and perturbation every 20, but if it doesn't improve, `no_acc` continues; after 100 break. good.

### Could `smooth_mutation` with scale 0.03 be too small; okay.

### In basin, after no improvement, scale increases. If scale becomes large (0.6), mutation may be very disruptive; optimize may recover. Accept only improves.

### Potential population contains old local minima with worse obj; sorting and trim keeps top 5. good.

### Final code length okay.

Let's finalize.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for low-max self-convolution non-negative step sequences.

Algorithm summary:
  * Work on a fixed length (usually 16384) with unit sum. The objective is
    2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a))^2, scale invariant.
  * Multi-start from many smooth/geometric/coarse-resampled initializations.
  * Fast multiplicative flattening steps inspired by EM for KL divergence to
    flat/capped/inverse convolution targets.
  * High-accuracy local polish using an LSE-smoothed max objective in softmax
    log-domain variables, optimized with scipy L-BFGS-B.
  * Exact active-peak subgradient/line-search polish.
  * Basin hopping with smooth Fourier mutations, mixing/crossover, and coarse
    linear-programming directions from the old LP-inspired update.

The code returns a Python list of non-negative floats.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import scipy.fft as scipy_fft
import scipy.optimize


def candidate_key(item):
    return item[0]


def next_power(size):
    if size <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()


def fft_conv_self(a):
    """Full linear self-convolution via FFT, length 2n-1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = 1 << (2 * n - 2).bit_length()
    F = scipy_fft.fft(a, size)
    b = np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(F * F))
    b = b[:2 * n - 1]
    # Remove tiny negative numerical noise.
    return np.maximum(b, 0.0)


def sequence_objective(a):
    """Objective value using FFT convolution."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.nan_to_num(np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0), nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    n = a.size
    if s < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    b = fft_conv_self(a)
    bm = float(b.max())
    if not np.isfinite(bm):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * bm / (s * s))


def normalize_to_sum(a, S=1.0):
    """Clip to [0,1000] and rescale to sum S."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0), nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(a.size, S / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (S / s)


def project_simplex(v, S=1.0):
    """Project onto {x >= 0, sum(x) = S}."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    if S <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u > (css - S) / idx

    if not np.any(cond):
        theta = float(v.mean())
    else:
        pos = np.where(cond)[0]
        rho = int(pos.max()) + 1
        theta = (css[rho - 1] - S) / float(rho)

    w = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
        w *= S / s
    else:
        w = np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return w


def resample_sum1(a, n_out):
    """Resample a non-negative sequence to length n_out and normalize to sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if n_out <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.full(n_out, 1.0 / float(n_out), dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.nan_to_num(np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0), nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(n_out, 1.0 / float(n_out), dtype=np.float64)
    a = a / s

    if a.size == n_out:
        return a

    if a.size > n_out and a.size % n_out == 0:
        b = a.reshape(n_out, a.size // n_out).mean(axis=1)
    else:
        x_in = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
        x_out = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_out)
        b = np.interp(x_out, x_in, a)
        b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)

    b = np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    s2 = float(b.sum())
    if s2 <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s2):
        return np.full(n_out, 1.0 / float(n_out), dtype=np.float64)
    return b / s2


def cross_corr_a_r(a, r):
    """
    C_i = sum_j a_j r_{i+j}, i=0..len(a)-1.
    Used for gradients of convolution and multiplicative updates.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    r = np.asarray(r, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = 1 << (2 * n - 2).bit_length()
    Af = scipy_fft.fft(a, size)
    Rf = scipy_fft.fft(r, size)
    c = np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(np.conj(Af) * Rf))[:n]
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def em_step_from_b(a, b, mode, eta, T=None):
    """
    Multiplicative one-step update.

    mode 0: flatten toward uniform target.
    mode 1: shave current peaks with a capped target.
    mode 2: inverse-proportional target (soft flattening).
    """
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    n = a.size
    m = 2 * n - 1
    if m <= 0:
        return a

    bmax = float(b.max())
    if bmax <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(bmax):
        return a

    if mode == 0:
        w = np.full(m, 1.0 / float(m), dtype=np.float64)
    elif mode == 1:
        if T is None:
            T = max(1e-300, 0.95 * bmax)
        w = np.minimum(b, T)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if ws <= 1e-12:
            w = np.full(m, 1.0 / float(m), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            w = w / ws
    else:
        floor = max(1e-10 * bmax, 1e-300)
        w = 1.0 / (np.maximum(b, floor) + 0.05 * bmax)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if ws <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(ws):
            w = np.full(m, 1.0 / float(m), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            w = w / ws

    floor = max(1e-10 * bmax, 1e-300)
    ratio = w / np.maximum(b, floor)
    H = cross_corr_a_r(a, ratio)
    H = np.maximum(H, 0.0)
    hs = float(H.sum())
    if hs <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(hs):
        return a

    logH = np.log(np.maximum(H, 1e-300))
    logmean = float(logH.mean())
    fac_arg = eta * (logH - logmean)
    fac = np.exp(np.clip(fac_arg, -8.0, 8.0))
    a_new = a * fac
    return normalize_to_sum(a_new, 1.0)


def em_optimize(a, time_budget, rng=None):
    """Iterative multiplicative flattening with exact objective acceptance."""
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a, np.inf
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(5.0, float(time_budget))

    cur = sequence_objective(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = cur
    if not np.isfinite(best_obj):
        best_obj = np.inf

    eta = 0.25
    it = 0
    no_accept = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        it += 1
        b = fft_conv_self(a)
        mode = (it - 1) % 3
        T = None
        bmax = float(b.max())
        if mode == 1 and bmax > 0.0:
            T = max(1e-300, 0.95 * bmax)

        accepted = False
        for _ in range(4):
            a_new = em_step_from_b(a, b, mode, eta, T)
            obj_new = sequence_objective(a_new)
            if np.isfinite(obj_new) and obj_new <= cur - 1e-16:
                a = a_new
                cur = obj_new
                if obj_new < best_obj:
                    best_obj = obj_new
                    best_a = a.copy()
                accepted = True
                break
            eta = max(0.02, 0.5 * eta)

        if accepted:
            eta = min(0.7, eta + 0.03)
            no_accept = 0
        else:
            no_accept += 1
            if rng is not None and it % 20 == 0:
                a_p = smooth_mutation(a, rng, 0.03)
                obj_p = sequence_objective(a_p)
                if np.isfinite(obj_p) and obj_p < best_obj + 1e-10:
                    a = a_p
                    cur = obj_p
                eta = 0.20
            if no_accept > 100:
                break

    return best_a, best_obj


def smooth_positive_signal(n, rng, base=1.0, scale=0.2):
    """Generate a positive signal as base plus low-pass noise."""
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    fsize = 1 << (n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.zeros(fsize, dtype=np.complex128)
    max_mode = min(fsize // 4, 80)
    if max_mode > 1:
        ks = np.arange(1, max_mode + 1)
        amps = rng.normal(0.0, scale / (1.0 + 0.1 * ks), max_mode)
        phs = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, max_mode)
        F[ks] = amps * np.exp(1j * phs)
        F[-ks] = np.conj(F[ks])
    noise = np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(F, fsize))[:n]
    noise = noise - float(noise.mean())
    return np.maximum(base + scale * noise, 0.01)


def smooth_mutation(a, rng, scale):
    """Multiplicative smooth noise mutation, projected to the simplex."""
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a
    fsize = 1 << (n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.zeros(fsize, dtype=np.complex128)
    max_mode = min(fsize // 4, 64)
    if max_mode > 1:
        ks = np.arange(1, max_mode + 1)
        amps = rng.normal(0.0, scale / (1.0 + 0.05 * ks), max_mode)
        phs = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, max_mode)
        F[ks] = amps * np.exp(1j * phs)
        F[-ks] = np.conj(F[ks])
    noise = np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(F, fsize))[:n]
    noise = noise - float(noise.mean())
    noise = np.clip(noise, -10.0, 10.0)
    a_new = a * np.exp(noise)
    return project_simplex(a_new, 1.0)


def phase_mutation(a, rng, scale):
    """Fourier phase mutation, projected to the simplex."""
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a
    fsize = 1 << (2 * n - 2).bit_length()
    F = scipy_fft.fft(a, fsize)
    mag = np.abs(F)
    phase = np.angle(F)
    freqs = np.arange(fsize, dtype=np.float64)
    weight = 1.0 + freqs / (0.1 * fsize + 1.0)
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, scale * weight, fsize)
    Fnew = mag * np.exp(1j * (phase + noise))
    if Fnew.size > 0:
        Fnew[0] = F[0]
    a_new = np.real(scipy_fft.ifft(Fnew, fsize))[:n]
    return project_simplex(a_new, 1.0)


def make_initials(n, given, rng):
    """Generate diverse initial non-negative length-n sequences with sum 1."""
    inits = []
    if n <= 0:
        return inits

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)

    if given is not None:
        g = np.asarray(given, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        g = np.nan_to_num(np.clip(g, 0.0, 1000.0), nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
        if g.size > 0 and float(g.sum()) > 0.0:
            g = g / float(g.sum())
            if g.size == n:
                inits.append(g.copy())
            else:
                inits.append(resample_sum1(g, n))
            if g.size != n and n > g.size and n % g.size == 0:
                rep = np.repeat(g, n // g.size)
                inits.append(normalize_to_sum(rep, 1.0))

    # Basic constant.
    inits.append(np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64))

    # Geometric / exponential families.
    lams = [0.3, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0]
    for lam in lams:
        a = np.exp(-lam * x)
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0))
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a[::-1].copy(), 1.0))

        a = np.exp(lam * (x - 0.5))
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0))
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a[::-1].copy(), 1.0))

        a = np.exp(lam * np.abs(x - 0.5))
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0))

        a = np.exp(-lam * np.abs(x - 0.5))
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0))

    # Endpoint power-law U shapes and center shapes.
    eps = 1e-6
    for p in [0.2, 0.5, 0.8]:
        a = (x + eps) ** (-p) + ((1.0 - x) + eps) ** (-p)
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0))
        a = (x + eps) ** p * ((1.0 - x) + eps) ** p
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0))

    # Windows.
    for p in [0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]:
        a = np.sin(np.pi * x) ** p + 1e-6
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0))

    a = 0.5 + 0.5 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)
    inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0))
    a = 0.5 - 0.5 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)
    inits.append(normalize_to_sum(np.maximum(a, 1e-6), 1.0))

    # Smooth random signals.
    for _ in range(4):
        a = smooth_positive_signal(n, rng, base=1.0, scale=0.3)
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0))

    # Random low-frequency blocks.
    for nb in [8, 16, 32]:
        bl = np.maximum(rng.normal(1.0, 0.4, nb), 0.05)
        a = np.repeat(bl, n // nb)
        if a.size < n:
            a = np.concatenate([a, np.full(n - a.size, bl[-1])])
        inits.append(normalize_to_sum(a[:n], 1.0))

    return inits


def softmax_from_z(z):
    """Stable softmax."""
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if z.size == 0:
        return z
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        return np.ones(z.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(z.size)
    m = float(z.max())
    p = np.exp(z - m)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(z.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(z.size)
    return p / s


def z_from_a(a, floor=1e-12):
    """Map a positive sequence to centered log-coordinates."""
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    aa = np.clip(a, floor, None)
    loga = np.log(aa)
    loga = loga - float(loga.mean())
    loga = np.clip(loga, -40.0, 40.0)
    return loga


def compute_lse_parts(z, n, beta):
    """Compute softmax a, convolution b, LSE value and weights."""
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if z.size != n:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        return None
    span = float(z.max() - z.min())
    if span > 800.0:
        return None

    a = softmax_from_z(z)
    b = fft_conv_self(a)
    if b.size != 2 * n - 1:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
        return None

    bmax = float(b.max())
    if not np.isfinite(bmax):
        return None

    M = b.size
    c = b - bmax
    maxc = float(c.max())
    beta = float(beta)

    if maxc < 1e-300:
        weights = np.full(M, 1.0 / float(M), dtype=np.float64)
        lse = bmax
        beta_eff = beta
    else:
        beta_eff = beta
        if beta_eff * maxc > 50.0:
            beta_eff = 50.0 / maxc
        scaled = beta_eff * c
        # scaled <= 0, cap for safety.
        scaled = np.clip(scaled, -50.0, 50.0)
        ex = np.exp(scaled)
        s = float(ex.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            weights = np.full(M, 1.0 / float(M), dtype=np.float64)
            lse = bmax
        else:
            weights = ex / s
            lse = bmax + float(np.log(s)) / beta_eff

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(weights)) or not np.isfinite(lse):
        return None

    return a, b, bmax, weights, lse


def full_lse_fun(z, n, beta, reg):
    """Objective: LSE-smoothed max(conv(a,a)) + tiny mean penalty."""
    parts = compute_lse_parts(z, n, beta)
    if parts is None:
        return np.inf
    _, _, _, _, lse = parts
    zarr = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(zarr)):
        return np.inf
    val = lse + float(reg) * (float(zarr.mean()) ** 2)
    return float(val)


def full_lse_jac(z, n, beta, reg):
    """Gradient of full_lse_fun z -> softmax(a) -> LSE(a*a)."""
    zarr = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    parts = compute_lse_parts(z, n, beta)
    if parts is None:
        return np.zeros_like(zarr)

    a, _, _, weights, _ = parts
    G = 2.0 * cross_corr_a_r(a, weights)
    dot = float(np.dot(a, G))
    grad = a * (G - dot)

    if np.all(np.isfinite(zarr)) and zarr.size > 0:
        m = float(zarr.mean())
        grad = grad + (2.0 * float(reg) * m / float(zarr.size)) * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    return grad


def lbfgs_polish(a, n, time_budget, betas=None, maxiter=30, maxfun=250):
    """L-BFGS-B polish in softmax log variables; returns best true objective."""
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a, np.inf

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(5.0, float(time_budget))

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = sequence_objective(a)
    a_work = a.copy()
    z = z_from_a(a_work)

    if betas is None:
        betas = [
            int(max(50, n // 4)),
            int(max(200, n)),
            int(max(500, 10 * n)),
        ]

    for beta in betas:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 12.0:
            break
        try:
            res = scipy.optimize.minimize(
                full_lse_fun,
                z,
                jac=full_lse_jac,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                args=(n, float(beta), 1e-10),
                options={
                    "maxiter": int(maxiter),
                    "maxfun": int(maxfun),
                    "ftol": 1e-15,
                    "gtol": 1e-10,
                    "disp": False,
                },
            )
        except Exception:
            break

        x = getattr(res, "x", None)
        if x is None:
            continue
        x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
        a_try = normalize_to_sum(softmax_from_z(x), 1.0)
        o_try = sequence_objective(a_try)

        if not np.isfinite(o_try):
            continue

        # Move along the LSE trajectory if it is not disastrous.
        if o_try < best_obj or o_try < best_obj * 1.25:
            a_work = a_try
            z = z_from_a(a_work)

        if o_try < best_obj:
            best_obj = o_try
            best_a = a_work.copy()

    return best_a, best_obj


def active_peak_polish(a, time_budget, k=16):
    """Active-peak subgradient polish for the true non-smooth objective."""
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a, np.inf

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(2.0, float(time_budget))

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = sequence_objective(a)
    cur = best_obj
    if not np.isfinite(cur):
        return best_a, best_obj

    step = 1.0
    kk = int(k)
    it = 0
    no_acc = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0 and it < 300:
        it += 1
        b = fft_conv_self(a)
        bmax = float(b.max())
        if bmax <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(bmax):
            break

        M = b.size
        kk_use = min(max(1, kk), M)
        idx = np.argsort(b)[::-1][:kk_use]
        vals = b[idx]

        floor = min(float(vals.min()), bmax - 1e-12)
        wv = np.maximum(vals - floor, 1e-300)
        ws = float(wv.sum())
        if ws <= 1e-300:
            wv = np.ones(kk_use, dtype=np.float64) / float(kk_use)
        else:
            wv = wv / ws

        wf = np.zeros(M, dtype=np.float64)
        wf[idx] = wv
        g = 2.0 * cross_corr_a_r(a, wf)

        accepted = False
        for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625):
            x = project_simplex(a - mult * step * g, 1.0)
            ox = sequence_objective(x)
            if np.isfinite(ox) and ox < cur - 1e-16:
                a = x
                cur = ox
                if ox < best_obj:
                    best_obj = ox
                    best_a = a.copy()
                accepted = True
                break

        if not accepted:
            step *= 0.5
            kk = min(128, kk * 2)
            no_acc += 1
            if step < 1e-5 or no_acc > 40:
                break

    return best_a, best_obj


def lp_direction(a_fine, n_c=512):
    """
    Coarse LP direction:
      maximize sum(g) subject to a_c * g <= 1,
    where a_c is scaled so that the current coarse self-convolution max is 1.
    The normalized g is automatically mixable with the fine sequence.
    """
    try:
        a_fine = np.asarray(a_fine, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        n_f = a_fine.size
        if n_f <= n_c:
            return None

        N = int(min(n_c, n_f // 4))
        if N < 64:
            return None

        a_c = resample_sum1(a_fine, N)
        b_c = fft_conv_self(a_c)
        M = float(b_c.max())
        if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
            return None

        a_lp = a_c / np.sqrt(M)
        A = np.zeros((2 * N - 1, N), dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(N):
            A[j:j + N, j] = a_lp

        c = -np.ones(N, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * N
        b_ub = np.ones(2 * N - 1, dtype=np.float64)

        try:
            res = scipy.optimize.linprog(
                c,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
            )
        except Exception:
            return None

        if getattr(res, "success", False):
            g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            if g.size == N and np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
                g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
                s = float(g.sum())
                if s > 0.0:
                    g = g / s
                    gf = resample_sum1(g, n_f)
                    if float(gf.sum()) > 0.0:
                        return gf
        return None
    except Exception:
        return None


def mix_two(a, b, t):
    """Mix two normalized sequences."""
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    b = normalize_to_sum(b, 1.0)
    if a.size != b.size:
        b = resample_sum1(b, a.size)
    c = (1.0 - float(t)) * a + float(t) * b
    return normalize_to_sum(c, 1.0)


def optimize_quick(a, time_budget, n, rng):
    """Fast polish used inside basin hopping."""
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(5.0, float(time_budget))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = sequence_objective(a)

    t = min(15.0, 0.35 * float(time_budget))
    if time.monotonic() + t < deadline:
        a1, o1 = em_optimize(a, t, rng)
        if o1 < best_obj:
            best_obj = o1
            best_a = a1.copy()
            a = a1
        else:
            a = a1 if a1.size == n else a

    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    t = min(20.0, 0.45 * rem)
    if t > 8.0:
        betas = [int(max(500, 10 * n))]
        a2, o2 = lbfgs_polish(a, n, t, betas=betas, maxiter=15, maxfun=120)
        if o2 < best_obj:
            best_obj = o2
            best_a = a2.copy()
        a = a2 if a2.size == n else best_a

    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    if rem > 2.0:
        a3, o3 = active_peak_polish(a, rem, k=12)
        if o3 < best_obj:
            best_obj = o3
            best_a = a3.copy()

    return best_a, best_obj


def optimize_candidate(a, time_budget, n, rng):
    """Deeper polish for a screened candidate."""
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, 1.0)
    if a.size != n:
        a = resample_sum1(a, n)
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(10.0, float(time_budget))

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = sequence_objective(a)

    # 1. Multiplicative flattening.
    t = min(30.0, 0.25 * float(time_budget))
    if time.monotonic() + t < deadline:
        a1, o1 = em_optimize(a, t, rng)
        if o1 < best_obj:
            best_obj = o1
            best_a = a1.copy()
        a = a1

    # 2. LSE L-BFGS with growing beta.
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    t = min(70.0, 0.4 * rem)
    if t > 20.0:
        betas = [
            int(max(50, n // 4)),
            int(max(200, n)),
            int(max(500, 10 * n)),
        ]
        a2, o2 = lbfgs_polish(a, n, t, betas=betas, maxiter=35, maxfun=300)
        if o2 < best_obj:
            best_obj = o2
            best_a = a2.copy()
        a = a2

    # 3. Second multiplicative pass.
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    t = min(20.0, 0.2 * rem)
    if t > 5.0:
        a3, o3 = em_optimize(a, t, rng)
        if o3 < best_obj:
            best_obj = o3
            best_a = a3.copy()
        a = a3

    # 4. High-beta LSE polish.
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    t = min(40.0, 0.4 * rem)
    if t > 10.0:
        betas = [int(max(500, 20 * n))]
        a4, o4 = lbfgs_polish(a, n, t, betas=betas, maxiter=25, maxfun=200)
        if o4 < best_obj:
            best_obj = o4
            best_a = a4.copy()
        a = a4

    # 5. Exact active-peak polish.
    rem = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    if rem > 3.0:
        a5, o5 = active_peak_polish(a, rem, k=16)
        if o5 < best_obj:
            best_obj = o5
            best_a = a5.copy()

    return best_a, best_obj


def get_target_length(budget_s):
    """Choose sequence length based on available time."""
    if budget_s >= 2100:
        return 16384
    if budget_s >= 1500:
        return 8192
    return 4096


def propose_candidate(seed=0, budget_s=2350.0):
    """
    Main search entrypoint. Returns list[float] of non-negative heights.
    """
    total_start = time.monotonic()
    budget = float(budget_s)
    deadline = total_start + max(60.0, min(budget, 2360.0))

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if np.isfinite(seed) else 0)

    print("propose_candidate start, budget=%.1fs" % budget)

    # Try to recover a previously known good sequence as fallback/initialization.
    given = None
    try:
        g = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        g = np.nan_to_num(np.clip(g, 0.0, 1000.0), nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
        if g.size > 0 and float(g.sum()) > 0.0:
            given = g
            print("Given sequence length=%d sum=%.3f" % (g.size, float(g.sum())))
    except Exception:
        given = None

    n = get_target_length(budget)
    print("Target search length n=%d" % n)

    # Fallback from the known global construction, at its original length.
    fallback_a = None
    fallback_obj = np.inf
    if given is not None:
        ga = normalize_to_sum(given, 1.0)
        go = sequence_objective(ga)
        if np.isfinite(go):
            fallback_a = ga
            fallback_obj = go

    # Initial candidates at target length.
    inits = make_initials(n, given, rng)
    cands = []
    cur_best_a = None
    cur_best_obj = np.inf

    for a0 in inits:
        a0 = normalize_to_sum(a0, 1.0)
        if a0.size != n:
            a0 = resample_sum1(a0, n)
        o0 = sequence_objective(a0)
        if np.isfinite(o0):
            cands.append((o0, a0))
            if o0 < cur_best_obj:
                cur_best_obj = o0
                cur_best_a = a0.copy()

    if cur_best_a is None:
        cur_best_a = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
        cur_best_obj = sequence_objective(cur_best_a)
        cands.append((cur_best_obj, cur_best_a.copy()))

    print("Initial best at n=%d obj=%.8f" % (n, cur_best_obj))

    cands.sort(key=candidate_key)

    # Optimize several top candidates with a deeper pipeline.
    topK = min(6, len(cands))
    phase_budget = min(1200.0, 0.55 * (deadline - total_start))
    if topK > 0:
        per_budget = max(20.0, phase_budget / float(topK))

        population = [(cur_best_obj, cur_best_a.copy())]
        for i in range(topK):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120.0:
                break
            a_init, o_init = cands[i]
            slot = min(per_budget, max(20.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 90.0))
            a_opt, o_opt = optimize_candidate(a_init, slot, n, rng)

            if o_opt < cur_best_obj:
                cur_best_obj = o_opt
                cur_best_a = a_opt.copy()
                print("Candidate %d improved: obj=%.8f (from %.6f)" % (i, o_opt, o_init))

            # Maintain population for basin hopping.
            if np.isfinite(o_opt):
                population.append((o_opt, a_opt.copy()))
                population.sort(key=candidate_key)
                if len(population) > 5:
                    population = population[:5]
            if cur_best_obj < fallback_obj:
                pass  # keep fallback for final decision
        population = [(cur_best_obj, cur_best_a.copy())] + [
            p for p in population if p[0] < cur_best_obj + 1e-9
        ]
        population.sort(key=candidate_key)
        population = population[:5]
    else:
        population = [(cur_best_obj, cur_best_a.copy())]

    # Basin hopping around the current best and population.
    print("Starting basin hopping, current obj=%.8f" % cur_best_obj)
    scale = 0.25
    lp_period = 3
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 80.0:
        it += 1
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem <= 0.0:
            break

        if population:
            population.sort(key=candidate_key)
            population = population[:5]

        if np.random.random() < 0.8:
            base = cur_best_a
        else:
            base = population[rng.integers(len(population))][1]

        r = rng.random()
        if r < 0.18 and len(population) > 1 and (it % lp_period == 0 and rem > 160.0):
            g_dir = lp_direction(base, n_c=min(512, n // 4))
            if g_dir is not None:
                t_mix = 0.10 + 0.30 * rng.random()
                cand = mix_two(base, g_dir, t_mix)
            else:
                cand = smooth_mutation(base, rng, scale)
        elif r < 0.38 and len(population) > 1:
            i1 = int(rng.integers(len(population)))
            i2 = int(rng.integers(len(population)))
            t_mix = 0.20 + 0.60 * rng.random()
            cand = mix_two(population[i1][1], population[i2][1], t_mix)
        elif r < 0.70:
            cand = smooth_mutation(base, rng, scale)
        else:
            cand = phase_mutation(base, rng, scale)

        q_budget = min(45.0, max(5.0, 0.25 * rem))
        a_opt, o_opt = optimize_quick(cand, q_budget, n, rng)

        if o_opt < cur_best_obj:
            cur_best_obj = o_opt
            cur_best_a = a_opt.copy()
            scale = max(0.05, 0.7 * scale)
            print("BH iter %d improved: obj=%.8f" % (it, cur_best_obj))
        else:
            scale = min(0.7, 1.15 * scale)

        if np.isfinite(o_opt):
            population.append((o_opt, a_opt.copy()))
            population.sort(key=candidate_key)
            if len(population) > 5:
                population = population[:5]

    # Final active polish if time remains.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic() - 5.0
    if rem > 5.0:
        a_pol, o_pol = active_peak_polish(cur_best_a, max(3.0, rem), k=32)
        if o_pol < cur_best_obj:
            cur_best_obj = o_pol
            cur_best_a = a_pol.copy()
            print("Final active polish improved: obj=%.8f" % cur_best_obj)

    final_a = cur_best_a
    final_obj = cur_best_obj

    # Choose between target-length result and known previous fallback length.
    if fallback_a is not None and fallback_obj < final_obj - 1e-10:
        final_a = fallback_a
        final_obj = fallback_obj
        print("Using previous fallback, obj=%.8f" % final_obj)

    print("Best internal objective=%.8f len=%d" % (final_obj, len(final_a)))

    # Convert to a numerically stable return scale. Objective is scale invariant.
    final_a = np.asarray(final_a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    final_a = np.nan_to_num(np.clip(final_a, 0.0, 1000.0), nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(final_a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        final_a = np.full(max(1, 4096), 1.0 / 4096.0, dtype=np.float64)
        s = 1.0

    # Scale to sum 100 for better direct-convolution numerical stability.
    final_a = final_a * (100.0 / s)
    mx = float(np.max(final_a)) if final_a.size > 0 else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
        final_a = np.full(final_a.size if final_a.size > 0 else 4096, 100.0 / 4096.0)
    elif mx > 1000.0:
        final_a = final_a * (1000.0 / mx)

    final_a = np.clip(final_a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    # Optional self-check for short sequences where direct evaluation is cheap.
    checked = None
    if final_a.size <= 8192 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
        try:
            checked = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in final_a])
        except Exception:
            checked = None

    if checked is not None and not np.isfinite(checked):
        print("Evaluation check failed; using uniform fallback.")
        final_a = np.full(4096, 100.0 / 4096.0, dtype=np.float64)

    elapsed = time.monotonic() - total_start
    print("Done in %.1fs. Returning length=%d" % (elapsed, final_a.size))

    return [float(x) for x in final_a]
```
